On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 12 v1.0

# The Mathematical Form of Sedimentation-Dynamics Concretization

Section 1  
Purpose

### 1.1 What Problem This System Solves

*The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* (Form Chapter 20 v1.2) sets up three structural quantities on an abstract working domain M: Differential Accumulation D (a difference of Sedimentation-Degree is present), Continuation Bias B (when the Sedimentation-Degrees of the compared objects are levelled to a declared common level, with everything else unchanged, the subsequent relative progression changes accordingly) and Familiar-Route Locking F (on a Familiar Route, the route's own bias persists in every sub-segment). *The Mathematical Form of the Field* (Field Chapter 1 v2.7) gives Sedimentation-Degree δ, stable re-traversal Ω\_W, the Familiar Route, Structural Bias Bias, continuation tendency κ, and Law 5 and Theorem 17 of “the deeper receives the flow first”. All of these quantities presuppose that “Sedimentation-Degree is borne by the hosting mechanism”, but none of them says, for a concrete host model, which mechanism state bears Sedimentation-Degree; whether the levelling intervention can be realized and, if it can, which states it changes and which it freezes; by what kind of reading relative progression is compared; and when D, B, F and Ω\_W, Bias hold together on the same host and when they come apart.  
The abstract definitions cannot answer these questions directly. On aggregated paths one levelling target can have several write-backs, and the choice of write-back changes the truth value of B; an intermediate common level does not guarantee that every Structural Position lands inside its state interval; one comparison task can give opposite values of B under the first-opened reading and under the share reading; relaxation runs only when plasticity is on, and leaving out this switch gets the decay of the path-depth difference wrong. On four declared host models, this system gives the exact form of these correspondences, executable evaluation procedures and the conditions under which they hold.

### 1.2 The Structure Formalized

The structure formalized is  
　　S₁₂ = { D, B (B\_m, B\_m^q), F }, taken from Definitions 4 to 6 and Sections 1, 4, 5 and 6 of Appendix 1 of *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2,  
together with its definitional constituents: the declaration of Depth (path-level model, aggregation model), the path threshold rule, the comparison task T = (M, K, P, W, Y, m), the validity conditions of the common-level function m, the levelling intervention I\_m (dependent quantities recomputed, frozen quantities kept, unique write-back) and member precedence Lead\_q. This system establishes a formal system for it on the declared host family 𝔉:  
　　S₁₂ ⟼ M₁₂.  
The Form kind is Mathematical Form, of the equations type, within the mathematical group established by *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*. It is assigned per the Canon Chapters, Part Three, Chapter Two. Entry conditions: S₁₂ has been established by the proof principal form *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 (conditionally established: the note to Section 12 and Section 14 of that paper state that the necessity of D, B, F is given by definitions and propositions, while the minimality and completeness of the whole structure are unproved; this system uses only the definitions of D, B, F and their proved relations); every component has stable terminology (Section 2, 2.2).  
M₁₂ takes over from *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 the two layers R and F, the witness-preserving embedding J, the three-valued evidence domain and the read-back regime: the event witnesses, transition relations and duty atoms of a host are evaluated per D4–D21 of Section 3 of that paper, and wherever the Trace-Responses determine the occurrence, timing or position of an action, evaluation uses R-layer witnesses (8.2 (6) of that paper). What M₁₂ newly sets up on this foundation is: the correspondence of the structural quantities to the host's mechanism states, events and interventions; the host realization of the levelling intervention and its valid parameter domain; evaluation procedures and criteria for B and F; and their relation to the coupling quantities.

### 1.3 Boundary

The object domain 𝔉 consists of four declared hosts: Host A, the frozen SNN instance s1 (a program host, a member of the event-gated class 𝒦\_g of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0); Host B, Family A of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0 (a constant-head two-channel flow network); Host C, Family E of the same paper (a firing-rate neural network); Host D, the minimal two-path threshold model (the model in which the blocked example of Section 5 of Appendix 1 of *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* and its contrast are set). The family-level theorems additionally cover the members of 𝒦\_g that satisfy the listed antecedents, and the variants of Families A and E within the listed parameter domains.  
Parameters and states are rational; wherever “c ∈ \[min, max\]” or “every c” is written, the intersection with ℚ is meant. Windows are finite and inputs are deterministic. The conclusions are model conclusions; read-back on real hosts requires the antecedents of Section 8, which are supplied by host-side certification. The objects of M₁₂ are structural quantities at the representation level; distinguishability under observation protocols involves interventions and observation protocols and is a problem at another level (note after Theorem R-B).

Section 2  
Core Thesis

### 2.1 Central Content

The central content this system must express precisely is this: on declared hosts, Continuation Bias B and Familiar-Route Locking F are functions of the comparison task, the write-back rule and the valid parameter domain, not functions of the host's actual history.  
Four requirements follow. First, the evaluation of B\_m must carry the values that the host's transition relation takes off the actual trajectory; the complete actual witness history does not suffice to determine B\_m and F (Section 10, Theorem R-B). Second, upstream stipulates “when every member already equals the common level, the write-back is the identity” as a definitional requirement on aggregated write-back (fragment B5′); equal-depth identity guarantees B ⇒ D, a general uniform write-back does not (Proposition B-D; Section 11, 11.1), and on particular fibres a non-identity operation can be reading-equivalent to a valid write-back (Definition D20, Theorem S-INV). Third, the intervention under a specified write-back rule is defined only within the valid parameter domain; “m is valid as a function” does not imply “the I\_m produced by that m is defined”, and any claim made for every valid m needs, as a separate antecedent, that the intermediate interval be contained in the valid domain (Definitions D14, D16, Lemma M1). Fourth, the reading type is a load-bearing symbol: under the three kinds of reading—first-opened set, order of precedence and share—the truth values of B\_m differ in general (Theorem TH-3 and Section 11, 11.2).

Fragment B5′ and the formulations listed at the end of this section likewise come from *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* v1.2 as carried in the main book v0.7.8 (file SHA-256 3e9657f2d81da0cff42a304078f297dfbdaa2ffef93bca769c4143f82fdecee5).

A Structural Position denotes a local field structure that can sustain pulses, retain Trace-Responses, be deepened by continuation, take part in overlap, and form Gully-Lines, Terminal-Ravines, Line-Bundles and Domains.” Equation 2 “δₜ₊₁(x)=δₜ(x)+Dτₜ(x)−Eλₜ(x)”.

Brk\_W is a direct fact of structural integrity; it depends neither on route qualification nor on S.”

*Familiar Route Measure Equations* (Field Chapter 11 v1.2):

This chapter does not establish the relation between the two.”  
　C2　Section 11: H\_δ “(i-1) … all paths use a common function θ(q) = g(δ̄(q)), with g non-increasing in δ̄; (i-2) or it is verified directly that the compared paths satisfy the required cross-path threshold order”, “H\_δ is a bridging hypothesis of this chapter, not a conclusion derived from Field Chapter 1”; FlowShare “is defined only when 0 \< F\_Q(t) \< ∞”; “ContinuationBias ∝ ΔFamiliarity” is withdrawn, “‘Continuation Bias’ has been fixed by Form Chapter 20 v1.2 as the dependence B on sedimentation differences”.  
Formulations from the same original (key points, not verbatim): “idempotent” means m(c, …, c) = c; the values of member precedence Lead\_q were originally named “sole lead / tied lead / not leading”, and this system keeps the original names (Definition D10); the window of the blocked example is explicitly taken long enough (for every intermediate common level, the unobstructed path opens within the window).  
Terminology: Differential Accumulation D refers only to the difference of Sedimentation-Degree (B1) and is kept apart from Dτ of Equation 2 (the process sense of deepening caused by Trace-Responses, A0); Continuation Bias B and Structural Bias Bias are not identical (B2); Lead\_q takes the original names sole lead, tied lead, not leading; the Familiar Route and stable re-traversal Ω\_W are kept apart (A4, A6); κ is a derived quantity (A3). In the conductance coefficient gᵢ = g₀ + κzᵢ of constitutive antecedent C1 of Family A of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, κ shares its name with continuation tendency κ but differs in meaning; this system writes the former as κ\_cons.

### 2.3 Working Input

　　𝕀₁₂ = ( host, path reading, K, Depth aggregation, write-back rule, freeze list, P, W, Y, m, sub-segment division, criterion N, ε\_δ )  
The components: the host is a member of 𝔉 (including its state space and transition relation); the path reading specifies what counts as a path and what counts as an opening event (3.2); K is the set of compared objects (a finite set of paths); the Depth aggregation is path level or a mode of aggregation; the write-back rule is the rule of I\_m on aggregated K (Definitions D14, D15); the freeze list consists of the states independent of Sedimentation-Degree; P is the input protocol; W is a finite window; Y is the reading type (D7–D9); m is the common-level function; the sub-segment division, the habitual-travel and re-traversal criterion N and the differential threshold ε\_δ serve F, Ω\_W and Bias.

### 2.4 The Four Hosts

Host A: the frozen SNN instance s1. Its R-layer representation is the 𝒦\_g member G\_SNN of Proposition 1 of Section 3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0: 4 entry units, 2 gating (LIF) units, and 8 synapses as Structural Positions; λ = 3/4, θ = 1, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 1/2, A₊ = 1/8, A₋ = 1/16, ρ = 0, baseline b = 1/4, state interval \[lo, hi\] = \[0, 2\]; the per-beat execution order is that of Definition 2 of that paper: integration reads the pre-beat w; gating g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; eligibility traces are updated first; potentiation P⁺\_ij = A₊x′ᵢ when plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j; depression P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_j when plastic ∧ bap ∧ sᵢ; relaxation P^r\_ij = −ρ(w\_ij − b\_ij) when plastic (it reads the pre-beat w and does not depend on bap); clipping is a position-wise projection. Declared history: formation period (pattern A with teaching current (1, 0), rest, rest) × 6, then 8 rest beats, then the window W = beats 26–43, consisting of (A, rest, rest, B, rest, rest) × 3. State at beat 26: w00 = w10 = 350417/524288 (z ≈ 0.418367), all other synapses 1/4; v = (0, 109052421333219/2251799813685248); y = (37449/33554432, 0) (Appendix B.5, Part 0).  
Host B: Family A (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, 4.2). Two channels with the same entry and the same head ΔP; Qᵢ = (g₀ + κ\_cons zᵢ)ΔP; write φcQᵢΔt/ℓ; relaxation −γzᵢΔt into the loose-material store Lᵢ; store output min(Lᵢ, eQᵢΔt); seed dumps 2 A\_u into channel 1. Parameters g₀ = 1, κ\_cons = 1, c = 1/5, φ = 1/2, ℓ = 1, γ = 1/10, e = 1/5, z\_max = 10. The reading is the slime-mould tube reading (under the river-channel reading the budget runs in the opposite direction; end of 4.2 of that paper).  
Host C: Family E (same paper, 4.3). A presynaptic neuron drives two postsynaptic neurons through two synapses; yᵢ = β(w₀ + zᵢ)x; write ηxyᵢΔt; decay −γzᵢΔt; the receptor pool Rᵢ is budgeted per N5 (w = number of receptors × g₁); pair induces +2 S\_u at synapse 1. Parameters w₀ = 1, β = 1, η = 1/10, γ = 1/10, g₁ = 1/10.  
Host D: the minimal two-path threshold model. Two paths a, b under entry e; Depth\_x is a state; θ\_x = g(Depth\_x), g(d) = 1/(1 + d); the entry flow pressure p(t) is a declared non-decreasing sequence; blockage Brk is a frozen flag; τ\_x = min{t ∈ W: Brk\_x = 0 and p(t) ≥ θ\_x} (written ∞ if there is none). There are two run conventions: stop (halt as soon as some path opens) and keep (a path stays open once opened and the run does not halt; the share reading is defined under this convention).  
Declared augmentation (marked “aug” throughout): Family A⁺ additionally has a frozen blockage flag brkᵢ (gᵢ = 0 when brkᵢ = 1), used to realize Brk\_W and contrasts of the blocked type; it is not part of the original declaration of Family A in *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*.

Section 3  
Basic Objects and the Definition Sequence

### 3.1 The Definition Sequence

The components of S₁₂ and their definitional constituents are inherited per 2.2 and are not redefined. The following are the new objects at the host level.  
D1　Host contract: one complete declaration of the working input 𝕀₁₂, written 𝔐.  
D2　Parameterized levelling I\_c: for c ∈ ℚ, I\_c sets the Sedimentation-Degree of every member of K to c, lands it on the Structural Positions by the write-back rule, recomputes the dependent quantities by the rules of M, and keeps the states in the freeze list unchanged. I\_m := I\_{m(Depth\_K)}.  
D3　B set: C\_B(T) := {c ∈ C\_leg(T): Y(I\_c(M)) ≠ Y(M)}; C\_B^q likewise (with Lead\_q in place of Y).  
D4　Bifurcation reading (Host A): entry group A\_in = {inputs 0, 1} (pattern A); path j = {(i, j): i ∈ A\_in}, j = 0, 1; opening event = within a beat, sᵢ = 1 (∀i ∈ A\_in) and g\_j = 1.  
D5　Convergence reading (Host A): path A = {w00, w10}, path B = {w20, w30}, both converging on output 0; the firings of output 0 are attributed according to the entry pattern of the beat, and when A and B fall in the same beat the firing is not attributed.  
D6　Single-synapse reading (Host A): path j = {w0j} (path level); opening event = s₀ = 1 and g\_j = 1.  
D7　First-opened set Y\_first: the set of paths that open on the earliest opening beat within W (∅ if nothing opens).  
D8　Order of precedence Y\_order: the weak order of the paths that open within W by their first opening beat (ties grouped together), with the set of paths that do not open listed separately; only the relative order is read, not absolute beats.  
D9　Share Y\_share: the ratio of the throughputs of the paths; when the total is 0 the reading is undefined (the same domain condition as FlowShare in fragment C2), and B is recorded as “reading undefined”, not judged false.  
D10　Member precedence Lead\_q (values under their original names sole lead / tied lead / not leading): under the first-opened set, Y = {q} is sole lead, q ∈ Y with \|Y\| \> 1 is tied lead, and anything else is not leading; under the order of precedence, the value is assigned in the same way from the first group; under shares, q being the strict maximum is sole lead, q tying for the maximum is tied lead, and anything else is not leading; if the share is undefined, Lead\_q is undefined.  
D11　SYM (the symmetry condition of the bifurcation reading): the two outputs have the same λ, θ, v\_reset; at the start of W, v₀ = v₁ and y₀ = y₁; the non-K synapses satisfy w\_i0 = w\_i1 (i ∉ A\_in), with the same baselines and state intervals; within W the teaching currents satisfy I₀ = I₁.  
D12　ENT: the entry letter is either all open or all closed on A\_in.  
D13　P-A: before the first opening event in W, no output fires on a non-entry beat. NC: before the first opening event, there is no clipping event on the synapses in K.  
D14　R-shift (a write-back rule; the paths share no Structural Position): every Structural Position in path P is set to z′ₓ = zₓ + (c − Depth(P)). Under mean aggregation the new path depth is c; when all paths have equal depth, the rule is the identity. When paths share Structural Positions the rule does not apply, and I\_c is recorded as undefined (explicit rejection).  
　　Valid parameter domain:  
　　　　C\_leg(T) := \[min\_K Depth, max\_K Depth\] ∩ ⋂\_{P∈K, x∈P} \[ℓₓ − zₓ + Depth(P), hₓ − zₓ + Depth(P)\] ∩ ℚ,  
　　where ℓₓ, hₓ are the endpoints of the state interval of zₓ. For c within the intermediate range, I\_c under R-shift is defined ⇔ c ∈ C\_leg. C\_leg may be empty or a single point, and is in general a proper subset of \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ. The corresponding domain of R-uniform (D19) is \[min, max\] ∩ ⋂ₓ\[ℓₓ, hₓ\] ∩ ℚ; when all positions have the same state interval, it equals \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ (a path depth is a convex combination of the positions in the box). In the path-level model, R-shift simply sets the position to c, and the valid domain is the same.  
D15　Equal-depth identity (normative requirement): when every path depth on K equals c\*, the write-back rule maps the state to the original state. Fragments B5 and B5′ together stipulate what counts upstream as a valid write-back rule: declared in advance, unique result, equal-depth identity. R-shift is a valid write-back rule; R-uniform in general lacks equal-depth identity and is not a valid write-back rule.  
D16　“m is valid as a function” means that m: ℚ^K → ℚ satisfies the four conditions of fragment B5; “the I\_m produced by that m is defined” additionally requires m(Depth\_K) ∈ C\_leg. The former does not imply the latter.  
D17　Effective relaxation rate: ρ\_eff := ρ·\[plastic\].  
D18　u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t) := Σ\_{s=t₀}^{t} λ^{t−s}(1 − ρ\_eff)^{s−t₀}\[A\_in open at beat s\] (0⁰ = 1), where t₀ is the start of W.  
D19　Auxiliary comparison operation: R-uniform (every Structural Position in K is set to z = c) is used as a formal auxiliary operation, not as a levelling intervention in the upstream sense; the Y and B obtained with it are called auxiliary readings.  
D20　Reading equivalence: if two write-backs (rules or operations) give the same Y on the same T, they are called reading-equivalent on T. The value of B is decided by the valid write-back rule; an auxiliary reading transfers the value of a valid write-back rule only when it is reading-equivalent to that rule (RB-2).  
3.2 Host Declarations: Sedimentation-Degree, Paths, Opening, Threshold and Entry Flow Pressure  
Host A. Structural Position = synapse; δ ↔ z = w − b (*The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, Definition 1, “Trace-Response z\_ij = w\_ij − b\_ij”). Positions with z \< 0 (w ∈ \[lo, b)) are valid in 𝒦\_g but have no counterpart in *The Mathematical Form of the Field*; this is registered as a correspondence obligation. Within the window of s1, the two synapses from inputs 2, 3 to output 0 take z ≈ −0.0078 and −0.00013 over beats 29–37 (Appendix B.5, Part 1); every position in K has z ≥ 0 throughout.  
　Three path readings are each well formed. Bifurcation reading (D4): the two paths share the entry, the two gating units obey the same gating law, and the order-type readings have a direct first-opening comparison here (Theorem S-FORK). Convergence reading (D5): the two paths converge on the same gating unit and share one threshold, the counterpart of the “Terminal-Ravine” listed in fragment A0; the entries do not fall in the same beat, (i) and (ii) of C₌ fail, and only the share reading is taken. Single-synapse reading (D6): the path-level K = {w00, w01} shares its entry with the non-K synapses w10, w11; at beat 26, w10 ≠ w11, the gating arguments of the two paths contain asymmetric non-K terms, and by fragment B3 they are not the same threshold mechanism.  
　Threshold mechanism and threshold order: under the bifurcation reading the two outputs have the same gating law. For s1, SYM fails at beat 26 (v₁ \> v₀ = 0, y₀ \> y₁ = 0), so the threshold order is verified directly by the second branch of fragment B3: output 0 opens at beat 26; the gating argument of output 1 at beat 26 is 4830756891370153/9007199254740992 ≈ 0.5363 \< 1, and output 1 does not open within W (Appendix B.5, Part 2).  
Host B. Path = channel i; δ ↔ zᵢ (the cross-section of above-baseline structural material on the wall, dimension L²), path level; the entry head is shared ((i) and (ii) of C₌ hold); the flow rate is a linear function of zᵢ and there is no event of opening on reaching a threshold, so Equation 4 (iii), Law 5 and Theorem 17 have no object; only the share reading is taken. δmax ↔ z\_max.  
Host C. Path = synapse i; δ ↔ zᵢ (above-baseline conductance), path level; no threshold; only the share reading is taken. The receptor pool Rᵢ is not encoded (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, C-C (2)).  
Host D. δ ↔ Depth (path level); one and the same threshold mechanism g, strictly decreasing; p is the shared entry flow pressure; Brk is frozen. Under the stop convention the first-opened set and the order of precedence are taken; under the keep convention the share is taken as well (the ratio of the numbers of beats in W during which each path is open).

Section 4  
Equations and Criteria  
4.1 Host Equations of the Levelling Intervention  
Host D: Depth′\_x = c, θ′\_x = g(c); Brk and p are frozen.  
Host A: the Structural Positions in K are written by a valid write-back rule: for the single-synapse path level, w′ = b + c; for aggregated K, R-shift. R-uniform serves only as an auxiliary comparison operation (D19), and its auxiliary readings must be transferred to R-shift through reading equivalence (D20; the corollary of Theorem S-INV, Corollary 1 of Theorem S-FORK). v, x, y, the input sequence, the teaching current, plastic, bap and clipping are frozen; there are no explicit dependent quantities (the threshold θ is a constant, and Sedimentation-Degree enters only through the gating argument). The intervention is implemented as state rewriting in model mode (as in 3.3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: “the setting directly rewrites the state variables read by the process”).  
Host B: z′₁ = z′₂ = c; the store L, the input protocol and the blockage (aug) are frozen; Qᵢ and the write rate follow. The total amount of structural material changes by Σᵢ(c − zᵢ)ℓ, which must be added from, or removed to, outside B\_phys.  
Host C: z′₁ = z′₂ = c; the receptor pool follows by the write-back rule R-pool: Rᵢ′ = Rᵢ − (c − zᵢ)/g₁ (the pool is a quantity determined by Sedimentation-Degree; receptor conservation Rᵢ + wᵢ/g₁ is unchanged under instantaneous setting).  
4.2 Difference Recursion and Convolution Formula of the Bifurcation Reading  
Under SYM, ENT and NC, before the first gating event, the synapses in K update as  
　　w′ = w − ρ\_eff(w − b) − A₋ d\_y y\_j·\[sᵢ\]·\[plastic ∧ bap\],  
the difference eᵢ between the synapses from the same input to the two outputs satisfies eᵢ(t+1) = (1 − ρ\_eff)eᵢ(t), and the path-sum difference satisfies E(t) = (1 − ρ\_eff)^{t−t₀}E(t₀), E(t₀) = S₀(t₀) − S₁(t₀), where S\_j is the synapse sum of path j. The difference of the gating arguments satisfies the convolution formula  
　　vt₀(t) − vt₁(t) = E(t₀)·u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t).  
With plastic off the weights do not change (ρ\_eff = 0); with plastic on and bap off there is only relaxation.  
4.3 Difference Recursion of the Share Hosts  
Family A: d = z₁ − z₂, d(t+1) = (1 + k\_t − γ)d(t), k\_t = φcκ\_consΔP(t)Δt/ℓ; Q₁ − Q₂ = κ\_cons d ΔP. Family E: k\_t = ηβx(t)²Δt, y₁ − y₂ = βxd.  
4.4 Opening Criterion of the Two-Path Threshold Model  
τ\_x is as in 2.4; Y\_first and Y\_order are given by τ; under the keep convention the throughput is n\_x = \|W\| − τ\_x (0 if the path does not open).

Section 5  
Running and Solving

### 5.1 Evaluation Procedures

B\_m(T): one actual run and one rerun under I\_m. All four hosts are deterministic single-history models (in Theorem 3 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, the number of successors per beat is 1); at a fixed state dimension, the number of arithmetic operations and the number of transition calls are linear in \|W\| (the bit length of the rationals may grow, so this is not linearity of bit complexity or of run time); the number of segments of the parameter machine is counted separately (5.5). The same holds for B\_m^q. F(q;T,W): the Familiar Route judgment (state and Pass) plus, for every sub-segment, one actual run and one I\_m rerun; the comparison task T\_k of sub-segment W\_k starts from the state that the actual run has reached at the start of W\_k, with K unchanged and window W\_k, and I\_m is applied at the start of W\_k. Ω\_W and Bias are obtained from the Pass, Brk and sub-segment counts of the actual run.

### 5.2 m-Family Reduction

Lemma M1 (m-family reduction, over ℚ). The m of this system are functions ℚ^K → ℚ. (i) I\_m depends on m only through c\* = m(Depth\_K); (ii) the set of values c\* taken by the m that are valid as functions is exactly \[min\_K Depth, max\_K Depth\] ∩ ℚ.  
Proof: (i) By the definition of I\_m. (ii) The intermediacy condition gives one inclusion. Conversely, the case min = max is trivial; otherwise, for c ∈ \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ take m\_α(δ\_K) = min + α(max − min), α = (c − min)/(max − min) ∈ \[0, 1\] ∩ ℚ: m\_α(c, …, c) = c, its value is intermediate, it depends only on min and max and is therefore symmetric, and it maps rational tuples to rationals. ∎  
Corollary: “I\_m is defined for every m valid as a function” ⇔ \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ ⊆ C\_leg. Under this antecedent, “B\_m is true / false for every valid m” ⇔ C\_B = \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ / C\_B = ∅. When the antecedent fails, only “B is true / false on the valid m within C\_leg” is reported, the segments outside C\_leg are listed as undef-Im, and no universal truth value is given. The program verifies the validity of m\_α (Appendix B.2, Part 1).

### 5.3 The Parameter Machine

For members of 𝒦\_g, c ↦ (gating record within W) is piecewise constant on C\_leg (Section 10, Theorem S-PAR). Algorithm: first compute C\_leg (Definition D14; reject on shared Structural Positions); output the part of \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ outside C\_leg as undef-Im segments; within C\_leg, advance beat by beat with w and v as affine functions of c and x, y as constants, split at the roots of the gating comparisons and of the clipping comparisons, and run the root points with exact values; in every open interval, take a random point and rerun it with exact values as a check. Only intervals within C\_leg are accepted; if an interval extends beyond C\_leg, the program refuses to run (Appendix A.8, legal\_domain, param\_B, param\_explore).

### 5.4 Deciding the Write-Back Set

The write-back set S(c\*) of a linear aggregation is a rational polyhedron; its nonemptiness and its coordinate projections are computed exactly by Fourier–Motzkin elimination, uniqueness is decided by whether every coordinate projection is a single point, and the result is checked against the tangent-cone criterion of Theorem AGG (2) (Appendix A.7).

### 5.5 Solvability

With a finite window and a finite state dimension, B\_m, B\_m^q, F and C\_B can all be computed exactly; the parameter machine has finitely many segments (at most n\_out + 2\|X\| roots per beat per segment). Stochastic and continuous input families, and implicit or continuous transition relations, lie outside the solvable domain (U12-4).

Section 6  
Coupling Relations  
6.1 With *The Mathematical Form of the Field* (Field Chapter 1 v2.7)  
δ: Host A, z (positions with z \< 0 have no counterpart); B and C, zᵢ ≥ 0; D, Depth.  
δmax: Host A, hi − b = 7/4, a constant upper bound; the state-dependent part “under the current field state” of fragment A1 has no counterpart; B, z\_max = 10; C, z ≤ y\_max/(βx) − w₀, given by y ≤ y\_max. The path-level I\_m and the auxiliary operation R-uniform take only intermediate values and need no clipping; the validity of R-shift is decided by C\_leg.  
Assignment to Equation 2 (per 6.1 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, “assigned by mechanism duty, not by sign”): Host A, PLUS → Dτ; RELAX → Eλ (in s1, ρ = 0, so it is absent); MINUS → undetermined (11.5); CLIP is not split under the joint reading and is recorded as R\_other under the separate reading. Host B: write → Dτ, relaxation → Eλ, seed dump → Dτ (pulse). Host C: Hebbian write → Dτ, decay → Eλ, pair → Dτ (pulse).  
Threshold and Equation 3a: under the bifurcation reading of Host A, with SYM and ENT, before the first gating event the difference of the gating arguments of the two paths satisfies the convolution formula (Theorem S-FORK (3)); the gating argument is an affine function of the initial path sum with coefficient u\_A^{ρ\_eff} ≥ 0; this is a direct first-opening comparison, not a proved realization of a shared entry flow pressure. D: θ = g(Depth).  
C₌ (fragment A7): under the bifurcation reading of Host A with SYM and ENT, (i), (iv) and (v) hold; the entry drive u\_A^{ρ\_eff} is in general not non-decreasing before opening (example in 10.9) and cannot serve as the shared entry flow pressure of (ii); the formal conditions of (ii) and (iii) can be met by the constructed equivalent representation (p̂, ĝ) (Lemma C-REP), but the correspondence of p̂ to an entry flow pressure of the host is not established (U12-10); for s1, SYM fails at beat 26; under the convergence reading (i) and (ii) fail; for B, (i) and (ii) hold while (iii) has no object; for D, C₌ holds when the paths are unobstructed.  
Law 5, Theorem 17: D has an object; the bifurcation reading of Host A has a direct first-opening comparison (Theorem S-FORK) and a correspondence under the formal equivalent representation (Lemma C-REP), with the mechanism-level correspondence of the entry flow pressure not established; the others have none.  
κ (fragment A3): does not enter into any evaluation; “κ does not reverse the δ order” is read as the monotonicity in Step 2 of Theorem S-FORK.  
Brk\_W: Host A, identically 0 (𝒦\_g has no disconnection mechanism, and synapse identity persists); B, realized by brkᵢ under aug; C, none; D, a frozen flag.  
Pass\_W: Host A, the number of opening events; B, the number of beats with Qᵢ \> 0; C, the number of beats with yᵢ \> 0; D, the number of open beats.  
Familiar Route: δ(q) \> 0, δ \> 0 at every Structural Position in the path (fragment C1), and Pass\_W ≥ N. Ω\_W: Pass\_W ≥ N\_Ω (taking N\_Ω = N), Brk\_W = 0, and passage events in every sub-segment. Bias: (4a) Depth\_q − Depth\_o \> ε\_δ at the start of W; (4b) operationalized as “in every sub-segment, q has more throughput than the other path”; the causal component “persistently biases later flow” of fragment A8 is not certified by this (RB-6).  
6.2 With *Familiar Route Measure Equations* (Field Chapter 11 v1.2)  
The path-level operationalization of the Familiar Route follows fragment C1. For the read-back of H\_δ (fragment C2), see the end of Theorem S-FORK. The domain condition of the share reading is the same as that of FlowShare.  
6.3 With *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* v1.0  
The event witnesses, R transition relation and duty atoms of Host A are evaluated in the R layer; the rerun for B\_m is a run of the same transition relation on the intervened state; Theorem R-B is a stronger form of Theorem 1 of that paper (Incompressibility of Mechanism Witnesses): B\_m and F are not determined by the witness history, and their evaluation must carry the transition relation itself.  
6.4 With *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0  
The R-layer encoding of Host A follows Definitions 1–7 and Proposition 1 of Section 3 of that paper; Appendix A.2 is the appendix program of that paper, and its SHA-256 is checked on loading (Appendix A.3). I\_m and the declared intervention domain 𝒟₁ of that paper are different intervention domains: 𝒟₁ sets only “available positions” (z \> ε and already formed), with values in \[b, hi\]; the two synapses of the shallow path of the bifurcation K of s1 have z = 0 and are not formed, and for c\* \> 0, I\_m must raise them, so I\_m ∉ 𝒟₁. B\_m and the functional dependence FD\_𝒟₁ of that paper are predicates on different intervention domains.  
6.5 With *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* v1.0  
The physical mechanism models, compilation and correspondence obligations of Families A and E are inherited per Section 4 of that paper and C-C; their encodings are isomorphic across the two families (Proposition 5 of that paper), and this system obtains the corresponding conclusion at the dynamics level (end of Theorems J-1, W-1).

Section 7  
Relation Properties  
R-1　“More familiar” (fragment A6, δ(a) \> δ(b) within K): irreflexive, asymmetric, transitive; not complete (paths of equal depth are incomparable). It is not declared a total order.  
R-2　Threshold order θ(x₁) \< θ(x₂): it runs in the same direction as “more familiar” when the shared threshold mechanism g is strictly decreasing; when the mechanisms are heterogeneous there is no such implication (Appendix B.2, Part 6). Irreflexive, transitive.  
R-3　“Precedes” (the strict order of opening times, with ∞ the largest): irreflexive, transitive, not complete. The weak order τ(x₁) ≤ τ(x₂) of Theorem 17(a): reflexive, transitive, complete (with the ∞ convention).  
R-4　No order on the values of Lead; “sole lead ⇒ belongs to Y\_first” holds in one direction only.  
R-5　“B\_m^q ⇒ B\_m” holds in one direction only and is not an equivalence (Appendix B.2, Part 7: Depth = (2, 0), m = min, B = 1, B^b = 0). “F ⇒ B\_m^q ⇒ B\_m” holds in one direction only (fragment B7); “B\_m ⇒ D” holds within the scope of Proposition B-D.  
R-6　The reading-coarsening relation “Y′ = φ∘Y”: reflexive, transitive; not antisymmetric (two readings that are functions of each other are coarsenings of each other), hence a preorder, not a partial order; Y\_first is a coarsening of Y\_order (take the first group).  
R-7　C\_B (c ranging over ℚ): on Host A (Theorem S-PAR) and on Host D it is a union of finitely many intervals and points; in general it is not an interval (the C\_B of the convergence reading of s1 consists of several segments, 11.6).  
R-8　Reading equivalence (D20): for a fixed comparison task and reading type, on the write-back rules or auxiliary operations whose compared readings are all defined, reading equivalence is reflexive, symmetric and transitive, hence an equivalence relation; it is in general not antisymmetric—different operations can give the same reading (witness: in 11.1 the non-identity uniform write-back and the identity write-back give the same reading)—and it does not imply that the written-back states are the same. Proof: reflexivity from Y = Y; symmetry and transitivity from the symmetry and transitivity of equality. The readings on both sides must be defined: two coinciding “undefined” marks do not count as reading equivalence.

Section 8  
Read-Back Rules

### 8.1 Direction

M → S: readings of M₁₂ are read back, through the antecedents below, to the structures of *The General Form of Sedimentation Dynamics* and *The Mathematical Form of the Field*. S → M: this system does not guarantee that the upstream structure has a faithful representation in M₁₂ on a given host (8.3).

### 8.2 Forward Read-Back

RB-1　D = 1 in M reads back the Differential Accumulation of fragment B1. Antecedents: the δ correspondence of 3.2 holds on that host (z is the Sedimentation-Degree borne by the hosting mechanism, fragment A1), and the mode of aggregation has been declared.  
RB-2　B\_m = 1 in M reads back the Continuation Bias of fragment B4 (for the same T). Antecedents: (a) the freeze list is exactly the set of states of the host independent of Sedimentation-Degree, and the dependent quantities are recomputed by the host's laws; (b) the reading type is declared in advance; (c) the value is taken from a valid write-back rule (fragments B5, B5′: declared in advance, unique result, equal-depth identity; on aggregated K, R-shift), and c\* ∈ C\_leg; a value obtained with the auxiliary operation R-uniform must be shown to be reading-equivalent to that valid rule (D20: the corollary of Theorem S-INV, or Corollary 1 of Theorem S-FORK), what is transferred is the value of the valid rule, and R-uniform does not thereby become a valid write-back; the universal conclusion “for every valid m” additionally requires \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ ⊆ C\_leg; (d) on a physical host, I\_m has a declared physical setting route and satisfies the resource conditions (Family E: beyond the resource condition of Theorem W-2, the setting operation must still be declared; Family A: the out-of-boundary setting of the declared augmentation). When these are not met, B\_m is only a reading within the formal model, and the B of the host is recorded as “physically undefined”.  
RB-3　B\_m = 0 in M reads back “the I\_m of this T does not change Y”; it does not read back that B is false under other m, Y or K. undef-Im and “reading undefined” are not read back as false.  
RB-4　F = 1 in M reads back the Familiar-Route Locking of fragment B7. Antecedents: the Familiar Route conditions of fragments A6 and C1 hold on the host, and the habitual-travel criterion and the sub-segment division have been declared; moreover, B\_m^q(T\_k) of every sub-segment satisfies all the antecedents of RB-2 (valid write-back rule or reading equivalence, c\* ∈ C\_leg, the additional antecedent of the universal criterion, physical setting route and resource conditions).  
RB-5　The relation of first-opened conclusions to Law 5 and Theorem 17 (fragment A9) has three layers. (A) Mathematical conclusion: Theorem 17 applies directly to the two-path threshold model when the paths are unobstructed; the bifurcation reading of Host A under SYM, ENT, P-A and NC (for any ρ ∈ \[0, 1\] ∩ ℚ and any plastic, bap, with formulas containing ρ read with ρ\_eff) has the direct first-opening comparison of Theorem S-FORK. (B) Partial correspondence with H\_δ: a correspondence of conclusion form (the deep path opens no later; the subthreshold condition for sole lead), see the end of 10.9. (C) Full C₌ read-back: it requires all the antecedents—a shared scalar p, a path threshold g, equivalence of opening, non-decrease before opening and so on; the entry drive u\_A^{ρ\_eff} is not non-decreasing before opening; the (p̂, ĝ) constructed by Lemma C-REP meets the formal conditions of C₌, so the conclusion of Theorem 17 applies under this formal representation, but the mechanism correspondence of p̂ as the host's entry flow pressure is not established (U12-10), and it is not read back that the host satisfies C₌. Share readings are never read back to Law 5. Families A and E and the convergence reading of Host A are not read back.  
RB-6　The values of Ω\_W and Bias read back fragments A4 and A8, with the operationalization of 6.1 as antecedent; the causal component “persistently biases” of Bias is not certified by the value of Bias, and the bearers of the cause are B and F (corollary of Theorem R-B).  
RB-7　Agreement with the proved structural propositions: Proposition 1 (B ⇒ D, fragment B6, whose premise contains equal-depth identity) holds under valid write-back rules by Proposition B-D (1), and holds likewise when auxiliary readings are transferred through reading equivalence; R-uniform is not read back where reading equivalence has not been established. D ≠ B, B ≠ F: every host has witnesses (Section 11). Law 5, Theorem 17: see the three layers of RB-5. “A Familiar Route does not presuppose Ω” (fragment A6): consistent with the “A4” witness of Family A⁺. “A Familiar Route need not be accompanied by Bias” (fragment C1): consistent with the equal-depth witness.

### 8.3 Reverse (S → M)

Whether an upstream B holding on a physical host can be detected by the I\_m of M depends on the fidelity of the host model and is not guaranteed by this system. The I\_m of Families A and E has a physical counterpart only once out-of-boundary setting operations have been declared. For real hosts such as riverbeds and fruit flies, the correspondence must be declared separately under the rules of this system.

### 8.4 Upper Bound of Read-Back

The antecedents of 8.2 are not produced by running this system. Without them, the readings of M₁₂ are only readings within the formal model, not structural facts on a real host.

Section 10  
Formal Realization Theorems  
The proofs in this section are all elementary: monotonicity, induction under symmetry, affine segmentation, linear algebra and polyhedra. The programs perform only finite verification and supply exact witnesses; universal conclusions rest on the proofs.  
10.1 Proposition B-D (B ⇒ D and the Write-Back Rule)  
(1) In the path-level model, or in the aggregation model with a valid write-back rule (fragments B5, B5′: equal-depth identity; R-shift is one such rule): B\_m ⇒ D on every contract.  
Proof: When D fails, Depth on K is everywhere equal to c₀, and by idempotence c\* = c₀. In the path-level model, I\_m sets each position to c₀, which is its original value; under R-shift, the shift c\* − Depth(P) is 0; in general, a rule with equal-depth identity maps the state to the original state. The dependent quantities, recomputed by the same rules, take their original values, and the frozen quantities are unchanged. Hence M after the intervention is the same as the original M, and Y is unchanged. ∎  
(2) The auxiliary comparison operation R-uniform lacks equal-depth identity; when the paths are internally non-uniform but of equal depth, it changes the state, and the auxiliary readings obtained with it can give D = 0 together with B = 1 (the example of 11.1). Equal-depth identity guarantees B ⇒ D; a general uniform write-back does not.  
(3) Sufficient antecedents for reading equivalence: if the paths of K satisfy the antecedents of Theorem S-INV (each path an entry group, the paths not overlapping, only the Structural Positions of K changed and the rest of the starting state the same, ENT, equal x within a group, the same d\_x, and no clipping event within K in W in either the actual run or the R-uniform run), then on states where D fails, R-uniform is reading-equivalent to the identity write-back, and the auxiliary reading gives B = 0.  
Proof: When D fails, the synapse sum of every path is Σ\_{x∈P} bₓ + \|P\|c\*, which equals the path sum after the R-uniform write-back; the two states lie in the same fibre of Theorem S-INV, the gating records coincide beat by beat, and Y is unchanged. ∎ Another sufficient antecedent is that of Theorem S-FORK (10.9 (2)).  
(4) On particular fibres a non-identity operation can be reading-equivalent to a valid write-back (the reading-equivalence example of 11.1: with fixed weights, R-uniform changes the state while the gating records are identical). Reading equivalence only shows that the auxiliary reading may transfer the value of a valid rule; it does not make the operation a valid write-back in the upstream sense (D19, D20, RB-2).  
Assigning B the value false directly when D = 0 would depart from the definition, “compare the actual reading with the intervened reading”, and is not adopted as an evaluation rule. The working domain is not restricted to nonnegative Sedimentation-Degree: synapses with z \< 0 are valid in 𝒦\_g.  
10.2 Theorem AGG (Definedness and Uniqueness of the Write-Back Set in the Aggregation Model)  
Let X\_K be the set of Structural Positions traversed by the paths of K, let the state box be 𝔅 = ∏ₓ\[ℓₓ, hₓ\], let A be a linear aggregation (its rows are the coefficients a\_{P,x} ≥ 0 of the paths), and let the write-back set be S(c*) = {z′ ∈ ℚ^{X\_K}: A z′ = c*·1, z′ ∈ 𝔅}.  
(1) I\_m has some linear write-back ⇔ S(c*) ≠ ∅. For the mean type (row sums equal to 1) with the same box at every position and c* ∈ \[ℓ, h\]: c\*·1 ∈ S. In general this is nonemptiness of a rational polyhedron, decided exactly by Fourier–Motzkin elimination.  
(2) Feasible-direction criterion. Let z₀ ∈ S. S = {z₀} ⇔ ker A ∩ T𝔅(z₀) = {0}, where T𝔅(z₀) = {d: dₓ ≥ 0 where z₀ₓ = ℓₓ, dₓ ≤ 0 where z₀ₓ = hₓ}.  
Proof: (⇐) If z₁ ∈ S and z₁ ≠ z₀, then d = z₁ − z₀ ∈ ker A; where z₀ₓ = ℓₓ, dₓ = z₁ₓ − ℓₓ ≥ 0, and where z₀ₓ = hₓ, dₓ ≤ 0; hence d ∈ T𝔅(z₀) \\ {0}. (⇒) If d ∈ ker A ∩ T𝔅(z₀) is nonzero, take the rational ε = min{1; (z₀ₓ − ℓₓ)/\|dₓ\|: dₓ \< 0; (hₓ − z₀ₓ)/dₓ: dₓ \> 0}; dₓ \< 0 forces z₀ₓ \> ℓₓ, and dₓ \> 0 forces z₀ₓ \< hₓ, so ε \> 0, and z₀ + εd ∈ S with z₀ + εd ≠ z₀. ∎  
(3) Corollary. When c\* lies strictly inside the box (ℓₓ \< c\* \< hₓ) and c*·1 ∈ S (mean type), T𝔅 = ℚ^{X\_K}, so S is a single point ⇔ ker A = {0} ⇔ rank A = \|X\_K\|. When c* lies on the boundary of the box, the rank condition is only sufficient, not necessary. Example: box \[0, 1\]³, rows of A (1, 0, 0) and (0, 1/2, 1/2), c\* = 0: rank A = 2 \< 3, yet S = {(0, 0, 0)} (ker A = span(0, 1, −1) meets the tangent cone {d ≥ 0} only at 0) (Appendices B.6, Part 1 and B.4, Part 2).  
(4) Bottleneck aggregation (Depth(P) = min\_{x∈P} zₓ). Every position belongs to some path, and if any position is \< c*, the depth of that path is \< c*; hence S(c*) = {z′ ∈ ∏\[c*, hₓ\]: every path has at least one position = c*} (c* inside the box). S is a single point ⇔ for every position x, c\* = hₓ or x belongs to some singleton path {x}.  
Proof: (⇐) Every position either has no room to be raised or is pinned to c\* by a singleton path, so S = {c*·1}. (⇒) If some position x has c* \< hₓ and every path containing x has length ≥ 2, then the configuration that raises only x to hₓ belongs to S (every path containing x still has another position at c*). Conversely, if S contains some z′ ≠ c*·1, take a position x with z′ₓ \> c*: every path containing x must have another position at c* (so its length is ≥ 2), and c\* \< z′ₓ ≤ hₓ. ∎ Equivalently: once a position is raised, every path containing it must still keep another position whose value is c*, and the position must actually have room to be raised. Example: K = {{x}, {y}, {x, y}} is unique both at c* = 1/2 and at c\* = h; “a path of length ≥ 2 makes it non-unique” is false (Appendix B.6, Part 1).  
(5) Under R-shift the write-back is given uniquely by the rule; it is defined ⇔ the paths share no Structural Position and c\* ∈ C\_leg. Parts (1)–(4) concern whether “the write-back set is nonempty and whether it is a single point”; R-shift is the point of that set specified by the rule: even when the write-back set is nonempty, R-shift can leave the box and so be undefined; failure of the rule means only that I\_m is undefined under that rule, not that every write-back is impossible. Example: with w00 = 0, w10 = 2 and all other synapses 1/4, the path depths of the bifurcation K are (3/4, 0); m = min gives c\* = 0; the uniform write-back c\*·1 lies in the box, but R-shift gives w00 = −3/4 (Appendix B.6, Part 5).  
Program check (Appendices B.4 and B.6, Part 1): in 1622 random linear instances, “every coordinate projection of the write-back set is a single point” agrees with (2), 0 violations; of these, the 518 instances with c\* interior and of mean type agree with the rank criterion, 0 violations; in 3000 random bottleneck path families, (4) agrees with exhaustive enumeration of the subsets of raised positions, 0 violations. For the K of s1 (state interval of z \[−1/4, 7/4\], c\* ∈ \[0, z₀\] ∩ ℚ strictly interior): the bifurcation and convergence K under mean aggregation traverse 4 positions with rank 2, and the write-back set is not unique; the single-synapse K is unique; for the nested K = {path A, input-0 branch, input-1 branch} under sum aggregation, the write-back set is empty for all three values of c\* (min, mean, max), and I\_m is undefined; the bifurcation K under sum aggregation has solutions, but not a unique one; under the bottleneck reading, the bifurcation K of s1 (two non-overlapping paths of two positions each) is not unique.  
10.3 Theorem S-INV (Reading Equivalence of Write-Backs on Entry-Group Paths)  
Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g; let every path of K consist of all synapses from an entry group Aₚ to some output jₚ, P = {(i, jₚ): i ∈ Aₚ}; let the Structural Positions contained in the paths of K not overlap; let ENT hold for every Aₚ; and let the inputs within the same Aₚ have the same d\_x and equal x at the start of W. Let two write-backs z, z′ change only the Structural Positions of K, leave the rest of the starting state (v, x, y and the non-K synapses) the same, and give every path the same synapse sum. If neither run has a clipping event on the K synapses within W, then the two runs have the same gating sequence, v, x, y and non-K synapses beat by beat, and the same synapse sum on every path beat by beat; hence, for any Y that reads only the gating record, the two write-backs are reading-equivalent on T.  
Proof: Induction on beats. x does not depend on w, so it is the same beat by beat in the two runs, and equal within a group beat by beat. Suppose that before a beat the two runs have the same v, x, y, non-K synapses and path sums. The gating argument is vt\_j = λv\_j + Σ\_{P: jₚ = j}(path sum)·\[Aₚ open\] + Σ\_{non-K} w\_ij sᵢ + I\_j; this is where non-overlap of the paths is used: the K synapses that output j reads in this beat are exactly the union of the paths of the open groups, and their total weight is the sum of those path sums. So the gating is the same, and so are v′ and y′. Potentiation P⁺ = A₊x′ᵢ (plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j) and depression P⁻ = −A₋y′\_j (plastic ∧ bap ∧ sᵢ) are the same for the same synapse in the two runs, and also the same for all synapses of the same group and the same output; relaxation runs only when plastic is on (independently of bap), reads the pre-beat value, and multiplies the above-baseline amount w − b of every synapse by (1 − ρ\_eff). The path sum therefore satisfies Σ′ = (1 − ρ\_eff)(Σ − Σ\_{x∈P} bₓ) + Σ\_{x∈P} bₓ + Σ(common increments), which depends only on the path sum itself and on increments that are the same in the two runs; without clipping, the two runs have the same path sums. The updates of non-K synapses read only x, y, g and the synapse's own position, and are the same in the two runs. ∎  
Note: the difference between the above-baseline amounts of two synapses in the same group is multiplied by (1 − ρ\_eff) at every beat; when the baselines within a group differ, the difference of the weights themselves does not scale in proportion (baselines (1/4, 1/2), weights (1, 1), ρ = 1/2, plastic on: weight difference 0 → −1/8, difference of above-baseline amounts 1/4 → 1/8). The proof uses only the path-sum recursion and does not depend on equal baselines within a group.  
“The paths do not overlap” cannot be omitted: three inputs and one output, letter A = (1, 1, 1), λ = 0, θ = 8/5, d\_x = d\_y = 1/2, A₊ = A₋ = ρ = 0, b = lo = 0, hi = 2, plastic off; K = {{w00, w10}, {w10, w20}}; under weights (1/2, 1/2, 1/2) and (3/4, 1/4, 3/4) both path sums are 1, the entries are synchronous and there is no clipping, yet after one beat of A the gating arguments are 3/2 and 7/4, with gating 0 and 1 (Appendix B.6, Part 6). With overlap, one must prove separately that the total weight of each actually activated set is determined by the path-sum constraints; this theorem does not cover that case. The rejection of shared Structural Positions by R-shift (D14) is a stipulation at the level of the rule and does not replace the antecedents of this theorem.  
Corollary (reading equivalence): R-uniform and R-shift fall in the same fibre (every path sum the same, only K changed); under the antecedents of this theorem, the auxiliary reading of R-uniform is reading-equivalent to that of the valid write-back R-shift, and what is transferred is the value of R-shift (RB-2).  
Item-by-item check of the antecedents: the bifurcation K of s1 (two paths {w00, w10}, {w01, w11} belonging to the two outputs, not overlapping, entry group {0, 1} satisfying ENT, x₀ = x₁) and the convergence K ({w00, w10}, {w20, w30}, disjoint entry groups {0, 1} and {2, 3}, x₀ = x₁, x₂ = x₃) satisfy all antecedents except the absence of clipping, which must be checked case by case; the single-synapse K is path level with a unique write-back and does not need this theorem; at every intervention point of s1 the paths are internally uniform, R-uniform = R-shift, and reading equivalence is not needed; in Proposition B-D (3) and in the randomized regression of Appendix B.6, Part 3, K consists of two non-overlapping entry-group paths with the rest of the starting state the same.  
Program check: for the convergence K, with random c\* and 400 random splits, none with clipping within W, the number of cases whose event sequence differs from that of the R-shift write-back is 0 (Appendix B.5, Part 5); in 1276 random cases of non-overlapping entry-group paths (including different baselines within a group), with only K changed, the rest of the starting state the same and no clipping, covering plastic × bap and ρ\_eff \> 0, the number of cases in which gating, v, x, y or the path sums disagree at some beat is 0 (Appendix B.6, Part 6). The clipping antecedent cannot be omitted: at the state of beat 26, with path A summing to 3/4, the splits (3/8, 3/8) and (0, 3/4), and P = A×3, the gating sequences are \[(0,0),(1,0),(0,1)\] versus \[(0,0),(1,0),(1,1)\] (Appendix B.5, Part 5).  
10.4 Theorem W-2 and the Mass Account of Family A  
Family E. If the receptor pool is frozen as a state independent of Sedimentation-Degree, every position with c\* ≠ zᵢ violates receptor conservation (*The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, N5), and whenever D holds every valid m is resource-invalid. The write-back rule R-pool recomputes the pool along with Sedimentation-Degree, as a quantity determined by it (4.1).  
Theorem W-2 (resource-feasibility condition under the R-pool rule). Under R-pool, the receptor pool after the intervention is nonnegative ⇔ c\* ≤ minᵢ(zᵢ + g₁Rᵢ). The set of resource-feasible c\* is \[min\_K z, min(max\_K z, minᵢ(zᵢ + g₁Rᵢ))\] ∩ ℚ; m = min is always resource-feasible.  
Proof: Rᵢ′ ≥ 0 ⇔ c\* ≤ zᵢ + g₁Rᵢ; at c\* = min z every position only decreases, so Rᵢ′ ≥ Rᵢ ≥ 0. ∎  
This condition is only the resource condition under the specified R-pool rule; having enough receptors is not the same as already having a valid physical setting route. The only out-of-boundary operation declared in *The Construction Form of Field Concretization* is pair (inducing a fixed +2 S\_u at synapse 1); there is no operation that sets a synaptic conductance to an arbitrary magnitude. A physical route for I\_m must be set up separately as a declared augmentation (aug), and none is currently declared. The pool does not enter the coefficients of writing and decay (Lemma 1, K2 of that paper), so the formal B\_m does not depend on the pool.  
Program (Appendix B.3, Part 1): for the state after the drive beat of H1 of the Construction Form, z = (21/10, 1/10), R = (19, 39), min, mean and max are all resource-feasible under R-pool, and none is feasible under the pool-frozen reading. For z = (2, 0), R = (20, 5): the resource bound is c\* ≤ 1/2; m = min and m\_{1/4} are feasible, mean and max are not (R′₂ = −5, −15).  
Family A. I\_m changes the total amount of structural material by Σᵢ(c\* − zᵢ)ℓ, which must be added from, or removed to, outside B\_phys (for z = (21/10, 1/10), min, mean and max give −2, 0 and +2 respectively). There is no direct physical connection between the walls of the two channels, so even the zero net exchange of m = mean cannot be realized inside the boundary. The only declared out-of-boundary action is a fixed dump of 2 A\_u into channel 1 (C5); in general I\_m requires an out-of-boundary setting that adds or removes arbitrary amounts, which must be declared separately (aug). Once it is declared, the budget closes with an out-of-boundary exchange term, c\* ∈ \[min, max\] ⊂ \[0, z\_max\], and the domain of validity is not exceeded. Within the current declaration only the special case z₂ − z₁ = 2, m = max is realizable.  
10.5 Theorem C-I (Classification of I\_m on the Four Hosts)  
Classification categories: \[well-defined and unique\] the target state exists, is unique and lies in the state space; \[well-defined but not unique, a rule must be declared\] the write-back set is nonempty but not a singleton, and becomes unique once a rule is declared; \[undefined\] no write-back satisfies the conditions, or under the specified rule the write-back leaves the valid domain, or the rule rejects on shared Structural Positions; \[formally well-defined but physically infeasible\] well-defined and unique in the formal model, but no realizable operation exists within the declared physical mechanism model, or the resource condition is violated.  
　D  Two-path threshold: path level, well-defined and unique; abstract model, so the physical question does not apply; B ⇒ D (Proposition B-D(1)).  
　A  SNN: single-synapse K at the path level, well-defined and unique. Bifurcation and convergence K with mean aggregation: the linear write-back set is nonempty but not a singleton (Theorem AGG(3)), so a rule must be declared; the valid write-back rule is R-shift (equal-depth identity), under which I\_m is defined ⇔ c\* ∈ C\_leg, and in general there exist m valid as a function for which I\_m is undefined (11.7: in 147 of 400 states uneven within paths, C\_leg is properly contained in \[min, max\]); R-uniform serves only as an auxiliary comparison operation, and under the antecedents of Theorem S-INV (including mutually non-overlapping paths and no clipping) its auxiliary reading is reading-equivalent to the R-shift reading. Nested paths of unequal length + sum aggregation: the write-back set is empty, undefined. On shared Structural Positions R-shift rejects, recorded as undefined. Under the bottleneck reading the s1 bifurcation K is not unique. The realization is state rewriting in the model mode of the program host and does not imply realizability on neuromorphic hardware or in biological tissue. The s1 instance: every intervention point is uniform within paths, R-shift = R-uniform, C\_leg = \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ — this is an instance property of s1, not a general fact about 𝒦\_g.  
　B  Family A: formally well-defined and unique; physically not realizable within the current declaration of *The Construction Form of Field Concretization*, which must be extended by an out-of-boundary setting, after which the budget closes with an out-of-boundary exchange term — this falls under “formally well-defined but physically infeasible (within the current declaration)”.  
　C  Family E: formally well-defined and unique; under the R-pool rule, resource-feasible ⇔ c\* ≤ minᵢ(zᵢ + g₁Rᵢ) (Theorem W-2), and under the pool-frozen reading resource-infeasible whenever D holds; no physical setting route is declared — this falls under “formally well-defined but physically infeasible (within the current declaration)”, with the resource bound given by Theorem W-2.  
Proof: for the individual items see 10.1–10.4, Theorem AGG and D14. ∎  
10.6 Theorem S-PAR (Exact Solvability of the B Set on 𝒦\_g)  
Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g, W finite (n beats), K arbitrary, and let the write-back be an affine function of c with slope 1 (both R-shift and R-uniform are), with valid write-back domain C\_leg (the intersection of a closed interval with ℚ, or empty, or rejection). Then c ↦ (gating record within W) is piecewise constant on C\_leg; the pieces are finitely many rational open intervals (intersected with ℚ) and isolated rational points, and their endpoints can be computed exactly; the pieces of \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ outside C\_leg are recorded as undef-Im and receive no true or false B. Hence, for any Y that reads only the gating record (first-opened set, order of precedence, share), C\_B and C\_B^q are, within C\_leg, finite unions of intervals and points and can be computed exactly.  
Proof: on a piece, w and v are affine functions of c, and x and y are constants. Base: within K, w = (constant) + c (the constant is w − Depth(P) for R-shift and b for R-uniform), and everything else is constant; the base step requires the initial write-back to lie in the box, i.e. c ∈ C\_leg. Induction: the gating argument vt\_j is affine; on the sub-pieces cut at the finitely many roots of vt\_j − θ\_j (each nonconstant affine function has at most one root), g is constant; so v′ (a constant on reset, vt otherwise) is affine, x′ and y′ are constants, P⁺ and P⁻ are constants, the relaxation is affine, and the pre-clip sum S is affine; then on the sub-pieces cut at the roots of S − lo and S − hi the clipping branch is constant, and w′ is a constant or S. At root points the run uses exact values. Each beat contributes at most n\_out + 2\|X\| roots per piece, so the number of pieces is finite. Outside the domain no intervention trajectory exists; clipping in the dynamics cannot retroactively validate an invalid initial write-back. ∎  
Program (Appendix B.5; Appendix B.6, Part 5): at a random point of each open interval the run is repeated with exact values; 0 violations in all cases. Degenerate case w00 = 0, w10 = 2: C\_leg = {3/4}; the parameter machine refuses to run on (0, 3/4), param\_B gives undef-Im for c = 0 and for (0, 3/4), and c = 3/4 is defined. Non-degenerate case w00 = 1/2, w10 = 3/2: C\_leg = \[1/4, 3/4\]; point by point at c = 0, 1/8 (outside the domain), 1/4, 3/4 (boundary) and 1/2 (interior), the definedness of I\_c agrees with the pieces of the parameter machine. Over 400 random states uneven within paths and 1458 checkpoints (min, max, both ends of C\_leg, random points inside the domain, points just outside it), 0 inconsistencies in definedness or readings; a K with shared Structural Positions is rejected in all three places: the valid domain, the concrete intervention and the parameter machine. Number of pieces on the actual window of s1: bifurcation 15, convergence 21, single synapse 9.  
10.7 Lemma CO and Theorem TH-3 (Reading Types)  
Lemma CO (monotonicity under coarsening). If Y′ = φ∘Y (φ a function), then for any m, B\_m^{Y′} ⇒ B\_m^{Y}; the two are equivalent when φ is injective on the relevant values.  
Proof: if Y is unchanged, so is φ(Y); take the contrapositive. ∎  
Corollary: Y\_first is a coarsening of Y\_order, so B^first ⇒ B^order on every host; Lead\_q is a coarsening of Y, so B^q ⇒ B (the one-way implication of fragment B7 thereby receives a uniform statement).  
Theorem TH-3 (two-path threshold model, keep convention). When the three readings are defined on both sides, B\_m has the same truth value under all three; the share reading is undefined ⇔ no path opens within W before or after the intervention.  
Proof: a path that has opened stays open, and its throughput is n\_x = \|W\| − τ\_x (0 if it never opens). Without blockage: τ\_a = τ\_b finite ⇒ after the intervention the threshold lies between the two thresholds and both paths open in the same beat (monotonicity), so none of the three readings changes; τ\_a ≠ τ\_b with at least one finite ⇒ Y\_first is a singleton, and after the intervention it is ∅ or the whole set (equal thresholds), so all three readings change (the share goes from ≠ 1/2 to 1/2 or to undefined); neither opens ⇒ neither opens after the intervention either (c\* ≤ max), the three readings are unchanged, and the share is undefined on both sides. With one path blocked: the blocked path opens on neither side, and whether each of the three readings changes depends on whether the opening of the other path changes; if it changes, the share is undefined on one side; if not, the shares are equal or undefined on both sides. ∎ Program check: 56700 values, 0 violations (Appendix B.2, Part 4).  
On Host A the B truth values of the share reading and of the order-type readings can disagree in both directions (11.2). The structural reason: in the two-path threshold model opening is a one-time persistent state, and the share is a function of the opening times; in 𝒦\_g opening is a repeated event with reset, and the share also depends on the dynamics after the first opening, so it is not a function of the order of precedence. The reading type is therefore a load-bearing symbol of B\_m, and B\_m conclusions under different reading types do not substitute for one another.  
10.8 Theorems TH-1 and TH-2 (First-Opening Criteria for the Two-Path Threshold Model)  
Theorem TH-1 (both paths unobstructed, one strictly decreasing threshold mechanism, p nondecreasing). For every valid m: B\_m^first(T) ⇔ Y(M) is a singleton ⇔ D ∧ P₁₇ ∧ the deeper path opens within W; B\_m^first does not depend on m.  
Proof: let Depth\_a ≥ Depth\_b. After the intervention both paths have the same threshold g(c\*) ∈ \[θ\_a, θ\_b\], so they open in the same beat or neither opens, and Y′ ∈ {∅, {a, b}}. If Y = {a}, then Y′ ≠ Y and B is true; Y = {a} holds exactly when Depth\_a \> Depth\_b, a opens within W and p(τ\_a) \< θ\_b. If Y = {a, b}, both paths open at τ; the post-intervention threshold, being ≤ θ\_b, is reached at τ and, being ≥ θ\_a, is not reached before τ, so Y′ = {a, b} and B is false. If Y = ∅, the post-intervention threshold ≥ θ\_a is not reached, Y′ = ∅, and B is false. ∎ This is a direct corollary of fragments B5 and B6 and Theorem 17. Program check: 6300 cases × 6 values of m, 0 violations (Appendix B.2, Part 2).  
Theorem TH-2 (a completely blocked). B\_m^first is true ⇔ \[p reaches g(Depth\_b) within W\] ≠ \[p reaches g(c\*) within W\]. Hence “false for every valid m” ⇔ p reaches g(min\_K Depth) within W or does not reach g(max\_K Depth); when max\_W p ∈ \[g(max), g(min)), B\_m varies with m, even under one and the same threshold mechanism.  
Proof: a opens on neither side; Y ∈ {∅, {b}}, and likewise Y′. The indicator \[max p ≥ g(c)\] is nondecreasing in c, and it is constant on \[min, max\] if and only if it takes the same value at both endpoints; Depth\_b is one of the endpoints. ∎ Program check: 3150 cases, 0 violations; 640 cases in which B varies with m under one threshold mechanism (Appendix B.2, Part 3). Witness A: the shallower a blocked, the deeper b unobstructed, Depth = (0, 3), p = (0, 1/5, 2/5, 2/5): B(min, mean, max) = (1, 0, 0). Witness B: the deeper a blocked, the shallower b originally not opening, Depth = (3, 1), the same p: B = (0, 1, 1).  
Relation to the blocked instance of fragment B6: the window of the blocked instance is taken long enough that the unobstructed path opens within the window for every intermediate common level (end of 2.2); this is exactly the antecedent of the first branch of Theorem TH-2, “p reaches g(min\_K Depth) within W”, so the two agree; without this antecedent B\_m varies with m. Fragment B5, “when the paths have different threshold mechanisms, the truth value of B\_m may vary with m”, lists one source; Theorem TH-2 gives another under the same mechanism: window truncation.  
10.9 Theorem S-FORK (First-Opening Criterion for the Bifurcation Reading on 𝒦\_g)  
Let G ∈ 𝒦\_g (ρ ∈ \[0, 1\] ∩ ℚ; run switches plastic and bap arbitrary; ρ\_eff = ρ·\[plastic\]), with two outputs j = 0, 1, entry group A\_in, K = {P₀, P₁}, P\_j = {(i, j): i ∈ A\_in}, mean aggregation, evaluated with the auxiliary comparison operation R-uniform (D19; when all positions have the same state interval its parameter domain is \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ, so it is defined for every m valid as a function), and Y = first-opened set (D7, with opening events as in D4). Assume SYM, ENT, P-A and NC (D11–D13). Then for every valid m the auxiliary reading satisfies:  
(1) B\_m^first(T) ⇔ Y(M) is a singleton; B\_m^first does not depend on m.  
(2) Y(M) is a singleton ⇒ D, and its element is the deep path; under the antecedents of this theorem the auxiliary reading also satisfies B ⇒ D.  
(3) (Direct first-opening comparison, convolution formula) Before the first gating event, vt₀(t) − vt₁(t) = E(t₀)·u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t), with u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t) ≥ 0. Hence the deep path opens no later than the shallow path; the deep path is the sole lead ⇔ at the deep path's opening beat the gating argument of the shallow path is \< θ. These two statements have the same conclusion form as Theorem 17(a) and (b); u\_A^{ρ\_eff} is not a shared entry flow pressure in the sense of C₌ (see the three layers of C₌ at the end of this section).  
Proof:  
　Step 1 (symmetry after the intervention). After R-uniform every synapse of both paths equals b + c\*; by SYM the non-K synapses are equal for the two outputs, v and y are equal, and so are the teaching currents. Induction on beats: equal gating arguments of the two outputs ⇒ identical gating ⇒ identical reset and y′; P⁺\_ij = A₊x′ᵢ does not depend on j, P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_j is the same for both outputs, relaxation gives equal components for equal weights, and the clipping bounds are the same — so all states of the two outputs agree beat by beat. Hence Y′ ∈ {∅, {0,1}}.  
　Step 2 (before the intervention, before the first gating event). Without gating events no potentiation occurs; y\_j only decays and is equal for the two outputs. The synapses in K are updated by the formula of 4.2: relaxation if and only if plastic, reading the pre-beat value; depression if and only if plastic ∧ bap ∧ sᵢ; with plastic off the weights do not change, and with plastic on but bap off only relaxation acts. By ENT, all inputs in A\_in open and close together, so the synapses in K receive equal depression in the same beats; by NC there is no clipping. Hence eᵢ(t+1) = (1 − ρ\_eff)eᵢ(t) and E(t) = (1 − ρ\_eff)^{t−t₀}E(t₀). The non-K synapses change identically for the two outputs. Without firing, v\_j(t+1) = vt\_j(t) and vt\_j(t) = λv\_j(t) + Σ\_{i∈A\_in} sᵢ(t)w\_ij(t) + (non-K drive, equal for the two outputs) + I(t), so Δvt(t) = λΔv(t) + s\_A(t)E(t) with Δv(t₀) = 0; induction on t gives Δvt(t) = Σ\_{s=t₀}^{t} λ^{t−s}s\_A(s)E(s) = E(t₀)·u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t). This gives (3).  
　Step 3. Until either run has had any gating, the intervention rerun shares x, y and the non-K synapses with the original run, whereas v in general differs, and the difference in v accumulates exactly from the difference of the synapses in K; v is the same at the start of W, and the difference between corresponding synapses in K is multiplied by (1 − ρ\_eff) at each beat, so vt*(t) − vt\_j(t) = (S* − S\_j(t₀))·u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t), with S\* = \|A\_in\|(b + c*) ∈ \[S\_shal, S\_deep\]. Lower clipping: w ↦ w − ρ\_eff(w − b) − δ is nondecreasing in w (1 − ρ\_eff ≥ 0), and in the intervention rerun every K synapse is ≥ the smallest synapse value in the original K (c* ≥ min\_K Depth ≥ the above-baseline amount of that smallest value); the latter is not clipped, so the former is not clipped at the lower bound either; upper clipping arises only from potentiation and does not occur before the first gating event. If Y = {deep}, then Y′ ≠ Y by Step 1, and B is true. If Y = {0, 1} (both open at τ, which by P-A is an entry beat): before τ, vt*(t) ≤ vt\_deep(t) \< θ, and at τ, vt*(τ) ≥ vt\_shal(τ) ≥ θ, so Y′ = {0,1} and B is false. If Y = ∅: by P-A there is no gating event within W; vt\*(t) ≤ vt\_deep(t) \< θ holds throughout, Y′ = ∅, and B is false. (2): if E(t₀) = 0, then Δvt ≡ 0, Y ∈ {∅, {0,1}}, and Step 3 again gives B false; if E(t₀) ≠ 0, then Y ∋ deep by (3). ∎  
Corollary 1 (reading equivalence with the valid write-back R-shift). Under the antecedents of this theorem, let c ∈ C\_leg (R-shift, D14), and suppose that in neither of the two intervention runs, under R-uniform and under R-shift, does a clipping event occur in K before that run's first opening event (denoted NC′). Then the two runs give the same first-opened reading; hence (1) and (2) of this theorem, stated for the auxiliary reading, hold for the valid write-back R-shift at these c, and what is transferred is the value of R-shift.  
Proof: the two runs differ only in the distribution within the paths of K; every path sum is the same, and all other starting states are the same; the two paths of K belong to different outputs and do not overlap, and the entry group A\_in satisfies ENT. Induction on beats up to the first opening event: suppose that before a beat the two runs have the same v, x, y, non-K synapses and path sums; the gating argument reads K only through the path sums, so the gating is the same; every synapse in K receives the same potentiation and depression components in the two runs (they read only x′, y′, g and s), relaxation multiplies the above-baseline amount by (1 − ρ\_eff), and by NC′ there is no clipping, so the path sums remain equal (this path-sum recursion is the same as in the proof of Theorem S-INV and does not use equality of x within a group). Hence the gating of the two runs agrees beat by beat before and at the first opening event, and the first opening beat and the paths that open in it are the same. ∎ Program check (Appendix B.5, Part 7): in random cases, taking both ends and one interior point of the valid domain of R-shift, 414 comparisons with NC′ satisfied, 0 with different first-opened readings.  
Lemma C-REP (formally equivalent representation of C₌). Under SYM and ENT, suppose that no clipping occurs in K before the first gating event of each output. For beat s write vt\_j(s) = a(s) + u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(s)·S\_j(t₀) (a(s) is the same for both outputs, Step 2; a(s) is computed by the auxiliary recursion with no gating, and once output j has had its first gating this auxiliary value is no longer taken as j's actual membrane potential; p̂ serves only for the formal representation of the first-gating time), and let  
　　σ\_s := (θ − a(s))/u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(s) (u \> 0); σ\_s := −∞ (u = 0 and a(s) ≥ θ); σ\_s := +∞ (u = 0 and a(s) \< θ),  
　　p̂(t) := −min\_{s≤t} σ\_s, ĝ(S) := −S.  
Then p̂ takes values in ℚ ∪ {±∞} and is nondecreasing in t, ĝ is strictly decreasing, and for each output j: output j has had a gating event at or before beat t ⇔ p̂(t) ≥ ĝ(S\_j(t₀)). Hence (p̂, ĝ) satisfies the formal conditions of C₌ (ii)(iii) (shared, nondecreasing before opening, opening if and only if its threshold is reached; Brk ≡ 0), and together with (i)(iv)(v), which SYM provides, the conclusion of Theorem 17 applies under this representation.  
Proof: while an output has not fired, its gating argument evolves only through its own synapses, v and y, and the common x and entry sequence, independently of whether the other output has fired; so vt\_j(s) = a(s) + u(s)S\_j(t₀) holds for s up to output j's first gating beat, and u(s) ≥ 0 (Step 2). Hence vt\_j(s) ≥ θ ⇔ S\_j(t₀) ≥ σ\_s (directly when u \> 0; by the choice of σ\_s when u = 0). Output j has had a gating event at or before beat t ⇔ ∃ s ≤ t: S\_j(t₀) ≥ σ\_s ⇔ S\_j(t₀) ≥ min\_{s≤t} σ\_s ⇔ p̂(t) ≥ ĝ(S\_j(t₀)). As the negative of a prefix minimum, p̂ is nondecreasing. ∎  
The entry drive itself is not nondecreasing before opening: two inputs and two outputs, λ = 1/2, θ = 10, v = x = y = 0, ρ = A₊ = A₋ = 0, b = 1/4, box \[0, 2\], both rows of weights (1/2, 1/4), history A, none, none: SYM, ENT, P-A and NC all hold, there is no gating and no clipping, and u\_A takes the values 1, 1/2, 1/4 in turn (Appendix B.6, Part 8). Program check of C-REP (Appendix B.6, Part 8): over 1245 random cases with SYM, ENT and no clipping in K (ρ, plastic and bap random), 0 violations of the prefix minimum being nonincreasing, and 0 inconsistencies in “first gating beat = min{t: p̂(t) ≥ ĝ(S\_j)}”.  
The three layers of C₌: (A) Theorem S-FORK and Corollary 1 are proved mathematical conclusions; (B) the correspondence with H\_δ is a partial correspondence in conclusion form (see below); (C) full read-back of C₌: Lemma C-REP gives a (p̂, ĝ) satisfying the formal conditions, but p̂ is derived from the gating dynamics, and its mechanism correspondence as the host's entry flow pressure is not established (U12-10); the entry drive u\_A^{ρ\_eff} cannot serve as the entry flow pressure of C₌.  
Only u\_A^{ρ\_eff} ≥ 0 and the symmetry of Step 1 are used, so (1), (2) and the two consequences in (3) hold for every ρ ∈ \[0, 1\] ∩ ℚ and every combination of plastic and bap.  
The relaxation term cannot be omitted: with ρ = 1/2, plastic on, λ = 0, one entry and two outputs, weights (1, 1/2), history none, A, the measured vt difference at the second beat is 1/4, the convolution formula gives (1/2)·(1/2) = 1/4, and the formula without relaxation gives 1/2; for the same state with plastic off the weights are constant, the measured value is 1/2, the convolution formula (ρ\_eff = 0) gives 1/2, and substituting ρ for ρ\_eff gives 1/4 (Appendix B.6, Parts 2 and 4).  
Endpoint: when ρ\_eff = 1 (ρ = 1 and plastic on; bap irrelevant), u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t) = λ^{t−t₀}\[A\_in open at t₀\]; if the entry is closed at the first beat of W, relaxation erases the path-depth difference at the first beat, Δvt ≡ 0, Y ∈ {∅, {0,1}}, and so D holds while B = 0. Weights (3/4, 1/2), ρ = 1, λ = 1/2, history none, A: with plastic on (bap on or off) the vt difference is (0, 0), with plastic off it is (0, 1/4); with history A, A (plastic on) it is (1/4, 1/8) (Appendix B.6, Parts 2 and 4).  
The antecedents cannot be dropped: without NC, unevenness between the two synapses of a path can let clipping reverse the order of the path sums (example: path 0 = (1/2, 1/2), path 1 = (0, 0.99); after a same-beat depression of 1/2 the path sums are 0 vs 0.49); without P-A, firing and reset at non-entry beats break the monotone comparison.  
Program checks: random check of the convolution formula (Appendix B.6, Part 2) — random SYM states (possibly uneven within paths), ENT, prefixes without gating and without clipping, the four plastic × bap combinations × ρ ∈ {0, 1/10, 1/3, 1/2, 3/4, 1}, 6810 beats in total, 0 violations; with ρ substituted for ρ\_eff, 1548 of the beats with plastic off and ρ ≠ 0 disagree with the measurement. Random check of the two-way conclusions (Appendix B.5, Part 7) — random members of 𝒦\_g (λ, θ, d, A₊, A₋, ρ ∈ {0, 1/10, 1/3, 1/2, 1}, plastic and bap random), SYM states, random protocols (including the letters B and AB and symmetric teaching currents), 220 valid cases (123 of them with D = 0), 0 violations.  
Partial correspondence with H\_δ (conclusion form): under SYM and ENT, expressed through the path depth δ̄ (mean), at actual comparison points where u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t) \> 0 the difference of the gating arguments has the same sign as the path-depth difference, and the effective threshold is strictly decreasing in δ̄; this corresponds in form to H\_δ (i-1) of fragment C2. The other path-comparison conditions contained in H\_δ are not given by this. At points where u\_A^{ρ\_eff}(t) = 0 (the entry drive has not yet appeared, or ρ\_eff = 1 with the entry closed at the first beat), only non-reversal of order is warranted, not strict decrease. For ρ\_eff \> 0 the contribution of the depth difference decays as (1 − ρ\_eff)^{s−t₀}.  
10.10 Theorem S-S1 (The Bifurcation Verdict for s1)  
For s1 at beat 26, with K\_bif, the actual window W, plasticity on (ρ = 0) and the first-opened reading: for every valid m, B\_m^first = 1 and B\_m^{q=path 0} = 1 (on s1, C\_leg = \[min, max\] ∩ ℚ, so the universal statement is defined; every intervention point is uniform within paths, R-uniform = R-shift, and the value is that of the valid write-back R-shift).  
Proof: the original reading is Y = {0} (3.2). SYM fails, so Theorem S-FORK does not apply directly, and we give a separate proof.
